Wilserdly Pill Nearly Doubled Time Without Progression in ESR1-Mutated Advanced Breast Cancer, Winning FDA Approval With Everolimus The FDA approved Wilserdly, or giredestrant, with everolimus for eligible adults with ESR1-mutated advanced breast cancer after a trial nearly doubled progression-free survival. It will not launch in the United States before the third quarter of 2028. Healthy Living Moderna RSV Shot 82% Effective Against Hospitalization in Veterans 50 and Older, but Infection Protection Fell With Age Published Nov 14, 2026 9:02 PM EST By Cole Mercer Scott Olson | Getty Images Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on WhatsApp Moderna's mRNA vaccine against respiratory syncytial virus was 81.8% effective at preventing RSV-related hospitalization among U.S. veterans 50 and older, according to a large Veterans Affairs study in JAMA Network Open reported by CIDRAP. Protection against any documented infection was lower, at 60.3%. Protection against infection also dropped steeply with age. It was 82.9% in veterans ages 50 to 59 but just 28.6% in those 80 and older. The findings arrive as RSV season builds across the country. For adult children helping an older parent decide on fall vaccines, the message is mixed but useful: the shot appears to sharply cut the risk of the most serious outcome, even if it does less to stop the oldest adults from catching the virus. Inside the VA Analysis of Nearly 75,000 Vaccinated Veterans Researchers compared 74,926 veterans who received a single dose of the vaccine, sold as mResvia, with 286,425 matched unvaccinated veterans. All were 50 or older and received care through the Veterans Health Administration. Vaccinations were tracked from Oct. 1, 2025, to Jan. 31, 2026, with up to 24 weeks of follow-up. The team used a target trial design, which tries to mimic a randomized trial using health records. Vaccinated veterans were matched to up to four unvaccinated veterans in four monthly sequential comparisons. Beyond hospitalization, the vaccine was 60.2% effective against RSV-related emergency department or urgent care visits. The authors said the results show effectiveness similar to that of protein-based RSV vaccines in earlier seasons. A previous VA study of those vaccines during the 2023-24 season found 78.1% effectiveness against documented infection in veterans 60 and older, according to MDedge's report on the earlier VA study. The FDA approved mResvia in May 2024 for adults 60 and older and expanded approval in June 2025 to adults 18 to 59 at increased risk of RSV lung disease. The new study is among the first large real-world looks at how the mRNA vaccine performs outside clinical trials. "Further follow-up is needed to assess the durability of mRNA-1345 protection over multiple seasons," the authors wrote. Evidence Check: Strong Signal, Real Limits The study is observational. Matching reduces, but cannot remove, differences between people who chose vaccination and those who did not. Veterans who use VA care are mostly older men, so the results may not fully apply to women or to younger, healthier adults. Earlier VA studies of the protein-based vaccines noted that RSV care received outside the VA system may be missed, a limitation that likely applies here as well. A prior VA analysis also found that protection from those vaccines waned over 18 months, with effectiveness against infection falling from 82.5% in the first month to 59.4% over the full period, according to MDedge's summary of the waning data. A separate VA analysis posted as a preprint, which has not been peer reviewed, estimated 87% effectiveness against RSV hospitalization using a different design, according to Preprints.org. That study was funded by Moderna, and eight of its authors are Moderna employees. Industry involvement does not invalidate results, but it is worth knowing when weighing them. The new study followed people for one season only. Whether mResvia's protection holds into a second or third season is unknown, a question that matters because the vaccine is currently given once, not every year. Older Adults and High-Risk Patients Have the Most at Stake Each year, an estimated 110,000 to 180,000 U.S. adults 50 and older are hospitalized because of RSV, according to the CDC's page on RSV in adults. Risk is highest for adults 75 and older and for people with chronic heart or lung disease or a weakened immune system. The CDC recommends a single dose of an RSV vaccine for all adults 75 and older and for adults 50 to 74 at increased risk of severe RSV, according to its clinical guidance for adult RSV vaccination. Adults who already received an RSV vaccine should not get another dose at this time. Uptake remains low. As of January 2026, 40.9% of adults 75 and older had received an RSV vaccine, according to an American Family Physician practice alert. Older adults hospitalized with RSV can face serious complications. A preliminary CDC analysis of 18,301 adults hospitalized with RSV over eight seasons found that 8.6% died in the hospital or within 30 days of discharge, U.S. Pharmacist reported. The age pattern in the new study is a reminder that the oldest adults may still catch RSV after vaccination. Early testing and treatment for symptoms, and extra precautions during peak season, still matter. Steps for Families This RSV Season Families can check whether an older relative has ever received an RSV vaccine, since only one dose is recommended. Pharmacies and primary care offices can look up vaccination records, and Medicare Part D generally covers recommended adult vaccines without a copay. Adults 50 to 74 can ask their clinician whether a condition such as chronic heart or lung disease, diabetes with complications, end-stage kidney disease, severe obesity, or a weakened immune system qualifies them. People who are unsure which RSV vaccine to choose can ask their clinician, since the CDC recommendation does not favor one licensed product. RSV often starts like a cold, with runny nose, cough, sore throat, and fever. Trouble breathing, wheezing, bluish lips, confusion, or worsening of heart or lung disease in an older adult need prompt medical care. Staying home when sick, washing hands and keeping sick visitors away from frail relatives still help, since vaccination lowers risk but does not replace these basics. The VA team said studies comparing different RSV vaccines and following people over several seasons would sharpen the picture, and MedicalDaily will report on those results. Read more Independent Reviewers Cannot Yet Say Whether Older Adults Need a Second RSV Dose This Coming Season RSV Vaccination Linked to 73 Percent Fewer Hospitalizations Among Nursing Home Residents in Large Medicare Analysis FDA Panel Backs Moderna's First mRNA Flu Shot — What It Could Mean for Your Fall Vaccine Key Questions Answered How effective was Moderna's RSV vaccine? It was 81.8% effective against RSV hospitalization and 60.3% effective against documented infection in veterans 50 and older. Did protection differ by age? Yes. Protection against infection was 82.9% in ages 50 to 59 and 28.6% in those 80 and older. Who should get an RSV vaccine? The CDC recommends one dose for all adults 75 and older and for adults 50 to 74 at increased risk of severe RSV. Do I need an RSV shot every year? No. The CDC currently recommends a single dose, and people already vaccinated should not get another. What are the study's limits? It was observational, covered one season and mostly included older men in VA care. What RSV symptoms need urgent care? Trouble breathing, wheezing, bluish lips, confusion or worsening heart or lung disease. 流感和呼吸道合胞病毒正在汉密尔顿传播。这是要做的事情

Flu and RSV are spreading in Hamilton. Here’s what to do
新闻简报安大略省汉密尔顿的甲型流感和 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 传播正在增加。据《汉密尔顿观察家》报道,截至 2026 年 11 月 7 日,甲型流感检测阳性率已稳步上升至 3.6%。汉密尔顿公共卫生部门还注意到 RSV 检测有所增加。作为回应,汉密尔顿健康科学公司和汉密尔顿圣约瑟夫医疗保健公司鼓励居民接种流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 疫苗,以最大程度地减少传播。他们建议生病时呆在家里,并在拥挤的室内空间戴口罩。符合条件的婴儿可以在麦克马斯特儿童医院接受 RSV 免疫接种。该文章没有提供具体的病例或死亡人数,但强调了人们日益增长的担忧,并呼吁社区采取行动以防止进一步传播。该信息截至发布日期 2026 年 11 月 17 日为最新信息。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 11 月 17 日,5 小时前更新。
地点与对象
加拿大安大略省汉密尔顿,提及汉密尔顿健康科学和麦克马斯特儿童医院。
事件
据《汉密尔顿观察家》报道,流感和呼吸道合胞病毒正在汉密尔顿蔓延,甲型流感确诊病例和呼吸道合胞病毒检测不断上升。
规模、影响与风险
11 月 7 日,甲型流感检测结果显示阳性率为 3.6%,RSV 检测结果也有所增加。证据中没有提供具体病例或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
医院敦促接种流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 疫苗,并建议生病时待在家里,并在拥挤的室内空间戴口罩。来源是一篇新闻文章,信息截至发布之日是最新的。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Flu and RSV are spreading in Hamilton. Here’s what to do

News Brief
Hamilton, Ontario, is experiencing an increase in the spread of influenza A and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). According to The Hamilton Spectator, the positivity rate for influenza A tests has steadily risen to 3.6% as of November 7, 2026. Hamilton public health is also noting a rise in RSV tests. In response, Hamilton Health Sciences and St. Joseph’s Healthcare Hamilton are encouraging residents to get immunized against flu, COVID-19, and RSV to minimize the spread. They recommend staying home when sick and wearing masks in crowded indoor spaces. Eligible infants can receive RSV immunization at McMaster Children’s Hospital. The article does not provide specific case or death counts but highlights the growing concern and the call for community action to prevent further spread. The information is current as of the publication date, November 17, 2026.

LM Otero photo By Joanna FrketichReporter Hamilton is seeing increasing spread of influenza A and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). The number of tests coming back positive for influenza A in Hamilton has been on a steady increase over the past three weeks to 3.6 per cent on Nov. 7 from 2.9 per cent on Oct. 31 and 2.2 per cent on Oct. 24. 免费 NHS 流感、新冠肺炎和呼吸道合胞病毒 疫苗 - 谁符合资格,谁不符合资格

Free NHS flu, COVID and RSV vaccines - who is and isn't eligible
新闻简报英国 NHS 正在向特定弱势群体(包括老年人、处于临床风险的儿童以及一线卫生和社会护理工作者)免费提供流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 疫苗。NHS 网站上概述了资格标准,其中多个 疫苗 的要求有些重叠。符合资格的个人可以在线预约或访问无需邀请的 疫苗接种 网站。该倡议旨在降低这些常见冬季病毒导致严重疾病的风险。虽然没有报告确切的病例或死亡人数,但 NHS 强调了年度 疫苗接种 对于高风险人群的重要性。该信息截至 2026 年 11 月 16 日为最新信息,没有提及有关该计划实施或结果的未解决的细节。

如 NHS 网站所述,NHS 向某些符合条件的群体免费提供流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 疫苗。这是一项公共卫生举措,旨在降低这些病毒导致严重疾病的风险。

Free NHS flu, COVID and RSV vaccines - who is and isn't eligible

News Brief
The NHS in the UK is offering free flu, COVID-19, and RSV vaccines to specific vulnerable groups, including the elderly, children at clinical risk, and frontline health and social care workers. The eligibility criteria are outlined on the NHS website, with some overlap in the requirements for multiple vaccines. Individuals who qualify can book appointments online or visit walk-in vaccination sites without needing an invitation. The initiative aims to reduce the risk of severe illness from these common winter viruses. While the exact number of cases or deaths is not reported, the NHS emphasizes the importance of annual vaccination for those at higher risk. The information is current as of November 16, 2026, and no unresolved details are mentioned regarding the program's implementation or outcomes.

Content cannot be displayed without consent Am I eligible for a flu vaccine? Adults at higher risk of getting seriously ill from flu and most children are eligible for the flu vaccine. If you're eligible, getting your flu vaccine every year is important, as it reduces your risk of getting severe symptoms: ‌ People aged 65 or over (including those who will be 65 by March 31, 2027) People aged 18 to 64 with certain long-term health conditions People who are pregnant People who live in a care home People who are the main carer for an older or disabled person, or receive a carer's allowance People who live with someone who has a weakened immune system People aged 16 or over and rough sleeping or staying in a homeless hostel or night shelter The criteria are slightly different for children, but most young people could be eligible: ‌ children aged 2 or 3 years (born between 1 September 1, 2022, and August 31, 2024) children aged 4 years who have not started school yet school-aged children (reception to year 11) children aged 6 months to 17 years with certain long-term health conditions Frontline health and social care workers will be offered a free flu vaccine through their employer. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

研究发现,疫苗 可为 50 岁以上成年人提供针对 RSV 的强大保护

Vaccine offers strong protection against RSV in adults over 50, study finds
新闻简报2026 年 10 月发表在《JAMA Network Open》上的一项研究发现,单剂 Moderna 的 mResvia RSV 疫苗 可为 50 岁以上的成年人提供针对 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的强大保护。该分析使用目标试验模拟框架进行,跟踪了退伍军人健康管理局 74,926 名 50 岁及以上接种疫苗的退伍军人的数据,并与 286,425 名对照个体进行匹配2025 年 10 月至 2026 年 5 月期间。24 周时,疫苗 对记录的 RSV 感染的有效性为 60.3%,对 RSV 相关急诊科或紧急护理遭遇的有效性为 60.2%,对 RSV 相关住院的有效性为 81.8%。然而,有效性随着年龄的增长而下降,从 50 至 59 岁人群的 82.9% 到 80 岁及以上人群的 28.6%。该研究强调需要进一步随访,以评估不同 RSV 疫苗 的长期保护和比较有效性。该研究结果基于 2025 年至 2026 年呼吸道疾病季节的数据,疫苗 于 2024 年 5 月获得 FDA 批准用于 60 岁及以上的成年人,并于 2025 年 6 月扩大批准用于 18 至 59 岁因 RSV 引起的下呼吸道疾病风险较高的成年人。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 10 月发表,分析的呼吸道疾病季节为 2025 年至 2026 年。疫苗 的有效性在 疫苗接种 后 24 周进行评估。
地点与对象
该研究分析了 74,926 名在退伍军人健康管理局接受护理的 50 岁及以上接种疫苗的退伍军人的数据,并与 2025 年 10 月至 2026 年 5 月期间的 286,425 名对照个体进行了匹配。
事件
在 50 岁及以上成年人中,单剂量的 mResvia RSV 疫苗 在 24 周时对记录在案的 RSV 感染的估计有效性为 60.3%,对 RSV 相关急诊科或紧急护理遭遇的有效性为 60.2%,对 RSV 相关住院的有效性为 81.8%。该研究是使用目标试验模拟框架进行的。
规模、影响与风险
疫苗 的有效性随着年龄的增长而稳步下降,从 50 至 59 岁人群的 82.9% 到 80 岁及以上人群的 28.6%。该研究没有报告具体病例或死亡人数,但重点关注对 RSV 相关疾病的有效性。
应对、状态与不确定性
需要进一步的随访来评估 mRNA-1345 保护在多个季节的持久性。该研究是一项使用目标试验模拟框架的 队列研究 研究,结果基于退伍军人健康管理局的数据分析。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Vaccine offers strong protection against RSV in adults over 50, study finds

News Brief
A study published in JAMA Network Open in October 2026 found that a single dose of Moderna’s mResvia RSV vaccine offers strong protection against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in adults over 50. The analysis, conducted using a target trial emulation framework, tracked data from 74,926 vaccinated veterans aged 50 and older in the Veterans Health Administration, matched to 286,425 control individuals between October 2025 and May 2026. The vaccine showed effectiveness of 60.3% against documented RSV infections, 60.2% against RSV-related emergency department or urgent care encounters, and 81.8% against RSV-associated hospitalizations at 24 weeks. However, effectiveness decreased with age, ranging from 82.9% among those aged 50 to 59 to 28.6% among those aged 80 and older. The study emphasized the need for further follow-up to assess long-term protection and comparative effectiveness of different RSV vaccines. The findings are based on data from the 2025 to 2026 respiratory illness season, and the vaccine was approved by the FDA in May 2024 for adults 60 and older, with an expanded approval in June 2025 for adults 18 to 59 at increased risk of lower respiratory tract disease caused by RSV.
Original source text (English)

That is according to an October study published in JAMA Network Open. “In this cohort study using a target trial emulation framework to evaluate mRNA-1345 vaccine effectiveness (VE), veterans aged 50 years and older vaccinated with a single dose of mRNA-1345 had a lower risk of having RSV-related illness during the 2025 to 2026 respiratory illness season than unvaccinated individuals,” authors said. “Further follow-up is needed to assess the durability of mRNA-1345 protection over multiple seasons, whereas comparative effectiveness studies can inform relative protection of different RSV vaccine products.” The study analyzed data from 74,926 vaccinated veterans 50 years and older receiving care in the Veterans Health Administration. The vets were matched to 286,425 unique control individuals between October 2025 and May 2026. Primary outcomes tracked included any positive RSV test result occurring from day 14 after the index date until the end of the study period or 24 weeks. Secondary outcomes included RSV-associated emergency department or urgent care encounters and RSV-associated acute care hospitalizations. The results showed that VE decreased steadily with increasing age. Specifically, VE was 82.9% among individuals ages 50 to 59, 66.3% among those ages 60 to 69, 64% among those ages 70 to 79 and 28.6% among those ages 80 and older. The FDA approved the vaccine in May 2024 for adults 60 and older; this approval was expanded in June 2025 to also include adults 18 to 59 who are at increased risk of lower respiratory tract disease caused by RSV. 儿科 毛细支气管炎 研究分析呼吸道病原体和医院康复时间

Pediatric Bronchiolitis Study Analyzes Respiratory Pathogens and Hospital Recovery Times
新闻简报中国北方石家庄市河北儿童医院进行的一项研究分析了 132 名两岁或以下住院婴儿中急性婴儿 毛细支气管炎 的病毒病因。该研究于 2026 年 11 月 12 日发表,发现 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 和人鼻病毒是最常见的病原体,分别占病例的 54.5% 和很大一部分。然而,在特定病毒特征或混合感染与医院康复时间延长之间没有发现统计学上显着的联系。该研究使用先进的分子诊断方法,包括多重逆转录聚合酶链反应测定,来检测病原体。尽管 112 名测试患者的检出率高达 88.4%,但作者指出,单中心样本量可能会限制检测恢复时间细微差异的能力。该研究强调需要进一步研究以了解各种病原体对幼儿住院结果的影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2022年9月至2025年2月进行,发表日期为2026年11月12日。回顾性调查于2026年完成。
地点与对象
这项研究是在中国北方石家庄的河北省儿童医院进行的。涉及132名两岁及以下住院婴儿,男女比例为2.3比1。
事件
一项回顾性研究分析了急性婴儿 毛细支气管炎 的病毒状况,确定 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 和人鼻病毒为主要病原体。研究发现,特定病毒特征或多微生物感染与医院康复时间延长之间没有显着相关性。
规模、影响与风险
Among 112 tested patients, RSV was the primary infectious agent, detected in 54.5 percent of cases. The overall pathogen detection rate was 88.4 percent, with 99 individuals testing positive for at least one respiratory organism. The median hospital length of stay was 6.0 days, with no significant differences in recovery duration linked to specific pathogens.
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究使用多重分子诊断分析和毛细管电泳片段分析来检测病原体。由于临床医生的判断力和资源限制,它排除了 20 名未接受检测的患者。作者警告说,单中心样本量可能会限制检测细微差异的统计能力。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pediatric Bronchiolitis Study Analyzes Respiratory Pathogens and Hospital Recovery Times

News Brief
A study conducted at Hebei Children’s Hospital in Shijiazhuang, northern China, analyzed the viral causes of acute infant bronchiolitis among 132 hospitalized infants aged two years or younger. The research, published on November 12, 2026, found that respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and human rhinovirus were the most common pathogens, accounting for 54.5% and a significant portion of cases, respectively. However, no statistically significant link was found between specific viral profiles or mixed infections and prolonged hospital recovery times. The study used advanced molecular diagnostic methods, including multiplex reverse-transcription polymerase chain reaction assays, to detect pathogens. Despite a high detection rate of 88.4% among 112 tested patients, the authors noted that the single-center sample size may limit the ability to detect subtle differences in recovery duration. The research highlights the need for further studies to understand the impact of various pathogens on hospitalization outcomes in young children.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 18, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Pediatric Bronchiolitis Study Analyzes Respiratory Pathogens and Hospital Recovery Times by November 12, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Pediatric Bronchiolitis Study Analyzes Respiratory Pathogens and Hospital Recovery Times Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A retrospective investigation conducted at Hebei Children’s Hospital in northern China has characterized the viral landscape of acute infant bronchiolitis, demonstrating that while respiratory syncytial virus and human rhinovirus represent the predominant infectious drivers, neither specific viral profiles nor polymicrobial infections significantly prolonged hospital recovery times. The clinical study, carried out in a tertiary-grade pediatric center located in Shijiazhuang, examined medical records spanning from September 2022 through February 2025 to determine how diverse viral agents influence hospitalization courses. Bronchiolitis remains the leading cause of acute lower respiratory tract infections requiring admission among children younger than two years of age, particularly presenting during the first six months of life with sudden-onset wheezing, cough, chest retractions, and auscultatory fine crackles. By evaluating clinical data from 132 hospitalized infants alongside advanced multiplex molecular diagnostic assays, the researchers sought to elucidate the local epidemiological patterns within a temperate, centrally heated continental climate and evaluate whether specific viral signatures altered hospital length of stay. Patient inclusion required children aged two years or younger who exhibited definitive clinical signs of acute bronchiolitis consistent with the 2024 edition of the Expert Consensus on the Diagnosis, Treatment, and Prevention of Bronchiolitis in Children, supported by respiratory specimen collection within twenty-four hours of admission. To isolate standard disease trajectories and minimize clinical confounding, investigators systematically excluded children diagnosed with congenital heart disease, bronchopulmonary dysplasia, underlying immunodeficiencies, or other severe systemic disorders, as well as individuals who received systemic corticosteroids or immunoglobulin therapy within one week prior to admission. The study cohort comprised 132 pediatric cases with an overall male-to-female ratio of 2.3 to 1, representing 92 boys and 40 girls. Across the entire cohort, the median age was 6.6 months, with infants under six months constituting 46.2 percent, children aged six to twelve months representing 37.9 percent, and toddlers between twelve and twenty-four months accounting for 15.9 percent. The overall median hospital length of stay was 6.0 days. Diagnostic assessment relied on multiplex reverse-transcription polymerase chain reaction assays paired with capillary electrophoresis fragment analysis, utilizing the GenomeLab GeXP Genetic Analysis System from Beckman Coulter. Specimen collection was conducted within twenty-four hours of hospital admission by dedicated clinical nursing personnel, who gathered posterior nasopharyngeal secretions using sterile synthetic-fiber swabs or deep lower respiratory tract secretions through negative-pressure suction into viral preservation solution. A commercial thirteen-pathogen panel developed by Ningbo Health Gene Technologies was utilized to simultaneously detect influenza A virus, influenza A 09H1, seasonal influenza A H3N2, influenza B, human adenovirus, human bocavirus, human rhinovirus, human parainfluenza virus, Chlamydia, human metapneumovirus, Mycoplasma pneumoniae, human coronavirus, and respiratory syncytial virus. Quality assurance protocols integrated human housekeeping genes as internal amplification controls alongside positive reference standards and negative controls to ensure specimen adequacy and rule out reaction inhibition. The authors noted that identifying pathogen nucleic acids confirms the presence of genetic material but cannot definitively distinguish active causative illness from asymptomatic carriage or prolonged viral shedding. Although the initial study protocol intended routine testing across all admissions, twenty of the 132 eligible patients did not undergo molecular diagnostic testing due to attending clinician discretion and variable resource availability. To prevent selection bias from skewing association analyses, investigators restricted all pathogen-outcome evaluations to the 112 tested patients while preserving the full 132-patient cohort for demographic descriptions. A dedicated sensitivity analysis was executed to determine whether systematic clinical divergences existed between the twenty untested children and the 112 tested counterparts. This comparative evaluation revealed no statistically significant differences regarding sex distribution, where males comprised 70.5 percent of the tested cohort versus 65.0 percent of the untested group, or the proportion of infants under six months of age, which was 48.2 percent versus 35.0 percent. Similarly, median age was 6.5 months among tested children compared to 8.8 months in the untested group, median hospital stay was 6.0 days versus 6.5 days, and rapid recovery rates were 46.4 percent versus 30.0 percent, indicating that restricting subsequent pathogen-recovery analyses carried minimal risk of distortion. Among the 112 children who completed molecular screening, the overall pathogen detection rate reached 88.4 percent, with 99 individuals testing positive for at least one respiratory organism. Single-pathogen infections were identified in 77 cases, accounting for 68.8 percent of tested children, whereas multi-pathogen mixed infections occurred in 22 patients, representing 19.6 percent of the tested cohort; thirteen children, or 11.6 percent, yielded negative results across the thirteen targeted pathogens. Respiratory syncytial virus emerged as the primary infectious agent, documented in 54.5 percent of tested cases, including 42 single infections and 19 mixed infections. Human rhinovirus represented the second most prevalent pathogen, detected in 31.3 percent of tested cases, comprising 19 single infections and 16 mixed presentations. Parainfluenza virus was identified in 6.3 percent of patients, presenting exclusively as isolated single infections. Human metapneumovirus was detected in 5.4 percent, influenza in 4.5 percent, Mycoplasma pneumoniae in 2.7 percent, human adenovirus in 1.8 percent, and human bocavirus in 0.9 percent. The combination of respiratory syncytial virus and human rhinovirus formed the most frequent mixed infection pattern, identified in eleven patients. Stratified evaluations revealed distinct age-related susceptibility patterns across the tested infant cohort. The detection frequencies for respiratory syncytial virus, human rhinovirus, and parainfluenza virus varied significantly across predefined age tiers of under six months, six to twelve months, and twelve to twenty-four months. Respiratory syncytial virus demonstrated elevated detection in younger cohorts, registering at 49 percent among infants younger than six months and 50 percent in those aged six to twelve months, before dropping significantly to 28 percent among toddlers aged twelve to twenty-four months. Human rhinovirus exhibited an even steeper age gradient favoring the youngest patients, appearing in 48 percent of infants under six months of age, but falling to 23 percent in the six-to-twelve-month group and 24 percent in the twelve-to-twenty-four-month bracket. Conversely, parainfluenza virus displayed an opposing trend, remaining rare among younger infants with a 1 percent detection rate in both the under-six-month and six-to-twelve-month groups, but increasing significantly to 10 percent among children aged twelve to twenty-four months. Pathogen activity demonstrated substantial seasonal variation within Shijiazhuang, characterized by a continental monsoon climate with cold, dry winters and prolonged centralized municipal heating running from mid-November to mid-March. This climate regime creates large indoor-outdoor temperature differentials and low indoor humidity, conditions known to enhance enveloped viral stability while drying mucosal barriers and impairing ciliary motility. Respiratory syncytial virus dominated the winter and early spring heating periods, reaching a monthly peak detection rate of 88.9 percent in February. Conversely, general hospital admissions declined during the summer and autumn months from June to October, but the relative positivity of alternative pathogens increased, with human rhinovirus reaching a peak proportion of 75.0 percent in June and parainfluenza virus reaching 66.7 percent in July. Intriguingly, an off-season epidemic surge of respiratory syncytial virus emerged in May, where overall pathogen testing yielded an 82.1 percent detection rate representing 23 positive cases among 28 tested patients. To evaluate whether this May epidemic represented a consistent annual pattern, researchers performed a descriptive sensitivity analysis tracking heating years from September to August. The data demonstrated that the May surge occurred almost exclusively during the 2022 to 2023 heating year, which recorded 27 tested patients in May with 23 testing positive for respiratory syncytial virus, alongside a diminished winter rate of 8.3 percent between December and March. In contrast, the subsequent 2023 to 2024 heating year recorded only a single tested patient in May, who tested negative, while exhibiting a pronounced winter wave from December to March where 22 of 27 tested children, or 81.5 percent, were positive for respiratory syncytial virus. The authors emphasized that because testing was not conducted as a continuous census and monthly enrollment numbers varied, these figures serve as descriptive sensitivity observations rather than evidence of a confirmed annual epidemiological shift. This temporal variation coincided with national adjustments in pandemic mitigation measures, leaving open whether the May surge represented an enduring regional phenomenon or a isolated post-restriction rebound. To assess clinical recovery speed, the investigators categorized patients using hospital length of stay, establishing a pragmatic threshold of five days or fewer for rapid recovery and greater than five days for delayed recovery. Within the 112 pathogen-tested patients, 52 children met criteria for rapid recovery while 60 experienced delayed recovery. Statistical comparison demonstrated no significant divergence between the groups regarding dominant viral pathogens or polymicrobial status. Respiratory syncytial virus was detected in 50.0 percent of rapid-recovery patients and 58.3 percent of delayed-recovery cases, human rhinovirus was present in 30.8 percent versus 31.7 percent, and mixed viral infections accounted for 13.5 percent versus 25.0 percent, respectively. While Mycoplasma pneumoniae and human bocavirus were detected exclusively in delayed-recovery cases, their low absolute frequencies precluded definitive association. Furthermore, while infants under six months represented 55.8 percent of the rapid-recovery group compared to 41.7 percent of the delayed-recovery cohort, this trend did not achieve statistical significance, illustrating that single or dual viral detections did not reliably predict prolonged clinical recovery. The authors emphasized that length of stay serves as a pragmatic operational metric influenced by complex non-biological factors, including parental anxiety, family economic circumstances, insurance mechanisms, clinician experience, and defensive medical practices aimed at preventing premature discharge or nosocomial exposure in vulnerable infants. Consequently, the lack of a statistically significant correlation between specific pathogens and recovery duration must be interpreted cautiously, as the single-center sample size limited statistical power for detecting subtle differences or analyzing low-frequency pathogens. Additional study limitations included the retrospective architecture, which precluded gathering standardized data on feeding practices, atopic predispositions, and secondhand tobacco smoke exposure, as well as the inherent diagnostic ambiguity between acute viral etiology and benign shedding. Nevertheless, because the overwhelming majority of documented bronchiolitis episodes were virally driven without clear evidence that multi-pathogen detection worsens recovery timelines, the researchers concluded that clinicians should strictly adhere to established antimicrobial stewardship principles, reserving empirical antibiotics and leveraging molecular testing to guide tailored supportive care. Flu, COVID-19 And RSV: What Marylanders Need To Know Before Thanksgiving - Patch

News Brief
The article, published on November 13, 2026, by Patch, discusses the current status of respiratory viruses in Maryland ahead of Thanksgiving. It notes that RSV-related hospitalizations are lower than in the previous season but still present, with the highest rates in Baltimore, Montgomery, and Prince George's counties. The piece emphasizes the importance of vaccination, especially for adults 75 and older, and highlights the need for precautions such as wearing masks and staying home when symptomatic. While the overall level of respiratory illness remains low, the article warns of the potential for transmission during family gatherings. It also mentions that the CDC recommends staying home until symptoms improve and using masks in crowded indoor spaces. The information is based on available data and recommendations, with some uncertainty about vaccine effectiveness. The article serves as a general guide for public health measures rather than a confirmed outbreak report.
Original source text (English)
Health & Fitness Flu, COVID-19 And RSV: What Marylanders Need To Know Before Thanksgiving Thanksgiving brings families together just as the flu and respiratory virus season gets underway. Here's what you need to know. Influenza-related hospitalizations and deaths are already being reported in Maryland as families prepare to gather for Thanksgiving, starting a season of togetherness that gives respiratory viruses plenty of opportunities to spread. Nationally, seasonal flu activity is increasing in many areas, while viruses that commonly cause colds are circulating at elevated levels, according to the latest update from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. COVID-19 activity is declining nationally, with emergency department visits low and decreasing. Still, wastewater monitoring shows moderate levels of the virus circulating nationwide, with some states reporting high levels. Find out what's happening in Across Marylandfor free with the latest updates from Patch. RSV, or respiratory syncytial virus, usually causes cold-like symptoms but can be serious for infants and older adults. Emergency department visits remain low nationally but are increasing in many areas. Wastewater readings for RSV and influenza A remain very low nationally. In Maryland, CDC data suggests the respiratory illness level remains low, as do cases in neighboring Virginia. In D.C., officials say the level is "very low." Find out what's happening in Across Marylandfor free with the latest updates from Patch. However, the Maryland Department of Health's dashboard indicates wastewater monitoring showed high levels of COVID-19 activity. As of Oct. 4, MDH reported a total of 150 COVID-19-related hospitalizations. Eight deaths were recorded in October. When it comes to the seasonal flu, MDH's dashboard notes minimal activity, with influenza-like illnesses accounting for less than 3% of emergency room visits during the last week of October. Since Oct. 4, a total of 254 flu-related hospitalizations were recorded, along with two deaths. From late August to the start of November, RSV-related hospitalizations in Maryland are little more than half of what they were during the same period in the 2025-2026 season. While the 2025-2026 season saw a total of 62 RSV-related hospitalizations, the 2026-2027 cases amount to 38. Available MDH data also shows Baltimore (21%), Montgomery (29%), and Prince George's (12%) counties accounted for the most RSV hospitalizations since Oct. 4. Vaccines Help, But Hurry Vaccines help, too, but it takes about two weeks for full protection to begin. So if you’re planning on getting a vaccination, now is the time to do it. Getting vaccinated later can still help protect against illness during the rest of the season. This season’s flu shot targets H1N1, the influenza A subtype causing most reported flu infections so far, along with H3N2 and an influenza B strain. Early laboratory testing suggests the vaccine is a good match for most H1N1 viruses tested, according to the CDC, although it’s too early to know how much protection that will translate into during this flu season. This season’s updated COVID-19 vaccines target XFG, a descendant of the Omicron variant, according to the CDC. The formula was selected to better match circulating viruses, but the shots don’t guarantee protection against infection. Their main benefit remains reducing the risk of serious illness, hospitalization and death. RSV vaccines help prevent serious illness from respiratory syncytial virus, which usually causes cold-like symptoms but can lead to pneumonia and hospitalization. The CDC recommends vaccination for adults 75 and older and those ages 50 to 74 at increased risk. Unlike flu shots, RSV vaccination isn’t currently annual: People who have already received a dose shouldn’t get another at this time. Check which flu, COVID-19 and RSV immunizations are recommended for your age and health. A pharmacist or other health care provider can help sort that out, and getting vaccinated often doesn’t require a separate doctor’s visit. Pharmacies, including CVS and Walgreens, offer the shots to eligible patients, generally at no cost with most insurance. Don’t Show Up Sick Nobody wants to miss Thanksgiving dinner, but showing up sick can send more than leftovers home with the family. The CDC recommends staying home and away from others until symptoms have been improving overall and you’ve been fever-free without fever-reducing medicine for at least 24 hours. For the next five days, take extra precautions because you may still be contagious. Hosts can help by bringing in fresh air. Open windows when practical, run a portable HEPA air cleaner or take some of the gathering outside if the weather cooperates. Testing before getting together can add protection, especially when visiting someone at higher risk. But a negative rapid test isn’t an all-clear if you’re coughing, feverish or otherwise feeling sick. A well-fitting N95 or KN95 mask can also reduce risk in crowded indoor spaces, during travel or around vulnerable relatives. The familiar advice still applies. Wash your hands, cover coughs and sneezes, and clean frequently touched surfaces. Get more local news delivered straight to your inbox. Sign up for free Patch newsletters and alerts

News Brief
The article discusses Chile's experience with a severe respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) outbreak during the winter season, which led to significant strain on pediatric healthcare resources. Juan Pablo Torres Torretti, a pediatrics professor at the University of Chile, notes the unusual situation of empty beds in pediatric wards and intensive care units, highlighting the severity of the impact. Chile reportedly took nine months to implement protective measures for infants, though the specific nature and effectiveness of these measures are not detailed in the evidence. The article does not provide exact case or death counts, but it underscores the challenges faced by the healthcare system due to the outbreak. The focus is on the delayed response and its implications for public health, without further specifics on the scale of the outbreak or the outcomes of the measures taken.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

Reflexive Respiratory Panels Could Reduce Full-Panel Testing by 27 Percent - Bioengineer.org

Reflexive Respiratory Panels Could Reduce Full-Panel Testing by 27 Percent - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报This news report discusses a study conducted by researchers at the Marshfield Clinic Research Institute and Marshfield Clinic, which evaluated the effectiveness of reflexive respiratory panels in reducing full-panel testing. The study analyzed 300 patient specimens from the Marshfield Clinic Health System in Wisconsin, collected during two distinct seasonal periods: April through June 2024 (low influenza-like illness) and January through March 2025 (elevated influenza-like illness). The findings indicate that implementing a customizable syndromic molecular panel with automatic reflex testing can reduce full-panel utilization by 27 percent while maintaining high diagnostic yields. This approach allows for tailored reporting to a baseline configuration of seven high-impact pathogens, including respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), and automatically reflexes negative samples to full-panel interrogation without requiring retesting. The study highlights the importance of molecular testing in guiding appropriate antiviral therapy and preventing unnecessary antimicrobial administration. The annual financial impact of upper respiratory tract infections in the U.S. exceeds twelve billion dollars, and the research underscores the need for healthcare laboratories to balance clinical necessity, operational feasibility, and financial sustainability when selecting diagnostic platforms. The results were verified through confirmatory bidirectional sequencing and compared against reference sequences in the National Center for Biotechnology Information database using BLAST.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究是在两个不同的季节期间进行的:2024 年 4 月至 6 月(流感样疾病低度)和 2025 年 1 月至 3 月(流感样疾病升高)。发布日期为2026年11月17日。
地点与对象
队列研究 研究包含来自威斯康星州北部和中部马什菲尔德诊所卫生系统的 300 名患者标本。这些样本是从具有扩展呼吸检测标准的患者身上采集的,例如入住 ICU、严重呼吸窘迫或全身免疫功能低下的患者。
事件
A retrospective evaluation demonstrated that implementing a customizable syndromic molecular panel with automatic reflex testing can reduce full-panel testing by 27 percent while maintaining high diagnostic yields. This was reported by researchers at the Marshfield Clinic Research Institute and Marshfield Clinic.
规模、影响与风险
该研究涉及 300 份样本,其中 150 份是在流感样疾病低度时采集的,150 份是在流感样疾病加重时采集的。研究结果表明,诊断管理可以在不影响病原体检测的情况下减少不必要的检测成本。在美国,上呼吸道感染每年造成的经济影响超过 120 亿美元。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是使用 BioFire Respiratory Panel 2.1 和 LIAISON PLEX Respiratory Flex Assay 的标准测试进行的。通过经过验证的两步裁决协议解决了不一致的结果。该研究的结果基于机构数据,与具体的 暴发 或 RSV 确诊病例无关。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Reflexive Respiratory Panels Could Reduce Full-Panel Testing by 27 Percent - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
This news report discusses a study conducted by researchers at the Marshfield Clinic Research Institute and Marshfield Clinic, which evaluated the effectiveness of reflexive respiratory panels in reducing full-panel testing. The study analyzed 300 patient specimens from the Marshfield Clinic Health System in Wisconsin, collected during two distinct seasonal periods: April through June 2024 (low influenza-like illness) and January through March 2025 (elevated influenza-like illness). The findings indicate that implementing a customizable syndromic molecular panel with automatic reflex testing can reduce full-panel utilization by 27 percent while maintaining high diagnostic yields. This approach allows for tailored reporting to a baseline configuration of seven high-impact pathogens, including respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), and automatically reflexes negative samples to full-panel interrogation without requiring retesting. The study highlights the importance of molecular testing in guiding appropriate antiviral therapy and preventing unnecessary antimicrobial administration. The annual financial impact of upper respiratory tract infections in the U.S. exceeds twelve billion dollars, and the research underscores the need for healthcare laboratories to balance clinical necessity, operational feasibility, and financial sustainability when selecting diagnostic platforms. The results were verified through confirmatory bidirectional sequencing and compared against reference sequences in the National Center for Biotechnology Information database using BLAST.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 18, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Reflexive Respiratory Panels Could Reduce Full-Panel Testing by 27 Percent by November 17, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Reflexive Respiratory Panels Could Reduce Full-Panel Testing by 27 Percent Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A retrospective evaluation conducted within a multi-hospital healthcare system demonstrates that implementing a customizable syndromic molecular panel with automatic reflex testing can maintain high diagnostic yields while reducing full-panel utilization by 27 percent. In an analysis of 300 sequential inpatient specimens collected across distinct seasonal periods of influenza-like illness, researchers at the Marshfield Clinic Research Institute and Marshfield Clinic evaluated the LIAISON PLEX Respiratory Flex Assay against the institutional standard-of-care BioFire Respiratory Panel 2.1. By tailoring reporting to a baseline configuration of seven high-impact pathogens and automatically reflexing negative samples to full-panel interrogation without requiring retesting, the clinical microbiology team demonstrated that diagnostic stewardship can curb unnecessary testing costs without compromising pathogen detection in symptomatic individuals presenting with acute upper respiratory tract infections. Upper respiratory tract infections remain the most prevalent global etiology of acute illness, imposing substantial clinical morbidity, mortality, and economic hardship across both adult and pediatric patient populations. In the United States alone, the annual financial impact linked to these common conditions exceeds twelve billion dollars, compounded during seasonal surges by respiratory viruses that frequently present with clinically indistinguishable signs and symptoms. During the 2024 to 2025 respiratory illness season, influenza alone generated between 610,000 and 1.2 million hospitalizations alongside an estimated 27,000 to 130,000 deaths nationwide. Because clinicians cannot reliably differentiate causative organisms based solely on bedside clinical presentation, definitive molecular testing using nucleic acid amplification tests or rapid antigen assays is essential to guide appropriate antiviral therapy, prevent unnecessary antimicrobial administration, and inform infection prevention and control practices. To navigate the expanding menu of molecular diagnostics, healthcare laboratories must constantly weigh clinical necessity, operational feasibility, and financial sustainability. Standard clinical laboratory practice frequently relies on maintaining separate platforms to accommodate varying levels of patient acuity and care settings. Low-risk outpatients seen in ambulatory clinics or urgent care settings are typically evaluated using four-target polymerase chain reaction panels restricted to SARS-CoV-2, Influenza A, Influenza B, and respiratory syncytial virus. Conversely, patients admitted to the intensive care unit with respiratory failure and multiple comorbidities may be tested on a syndromic panel covering the majority of possible respiratory infections. Verifying, maintaining, and quality-controlling multiple distinct platforms introduces substantial operational complexity, requiring duplicate validation protocols, divergent quality control schedules, and ongoing technologist competency assessments that place sustained demands on clinical microbiology resources. Discover more Research assistant services Postdoc opportunities Research report templates Learn Engineering Track Biotech The study cohort comprised 300 sequential patient specimens meeting institutional criteria for extended respiratory testing, such as intensive care unit admission, severe respiratory distress, or systemic immunocompromise, within the Marshfield Clinic Health System in northern and central Wisconsin. Testing was split evenly across two distinct seasonal epidemiologic windows designated by the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention surveillance metrics: a period of low influenza-like illness spanning April through June 2024 (n = 150) and a period of elevated influenza-like illness spanning January through March 2025 (n = 150). The mean patient age in the elevated cohort was 52.2 years, significantly younger than the 62.5-year mean documented during the low-activity cohort (p = 0.002), while patient sex distributions and underlying comorbid conditions—including active malignancy, diabetes mellitus, corticosteroid therapy, and solid organ transplantation—demonstrated no statistically significant differences between the two study periods. Specimens underwent standard testing on the BioFire Respiratory Panel 2.1 followed by evaluation on the LIAISON PLEX Respiratory Flex Assay within 72 hours of collection while stored at 4 degrees Celsius. When initial evaluations revealed discrepant results between the two multiplex platforms, samples underwent additional testing performed by Diasorin using a validated two-step adjudication protocol. Nucleic acids underwent multiplex fragment analysis targeting between one and four analytes using fluorophore-labeled primers resolved by capillary electrophoresis on an Applied Biosystems 3730xl Genetic Analyzer. Amplicons meeting size and fluorescence thresholds advanced to confirmatory bidirectional sequencing, where sequences achieving PHRED quality scores of at least 20 across 90 percent of bases and minimum 100 contiguous base lengths were compared against reference sequences in the National Center for Biotechnology Information database using BLAST. Discover more Tour Colleges Cancer research funding Donate to Research Initial concordance testing between the platforms yielded an unadjusted positive agreement of 84.8 percent and a negative agreement of 97.9 percent before discordant analysis. Five clinical specimens presented with a shared single positive target alongside a discordant secondary target and were excluded from comparative calculations because they met simultaneous criteria for concordance and discordance. Of sixteen specimens positive solely by the BioFire platform, confirmatory bidirectional sequencing verified the initial finding in only two instances, one specimen was omitted due to technical processing error, and twelve returned negative results. All four specimens initially identified as positive solely by the LIAISON platform tested negative upon referee sequencing. Following the removal of unconfirmed discordant samples, post-arbitration agreement resolved to 96.7 percent positive agreement across 92 validated positive samples and 100 percent negative agreement across 186 negative specimens. Epidemiological surveillance across the two study intervals revealed pronounced seasonal variations in circulating respiratory pathogens identified by the flexible multiplex assay. During the low influenza-like illness interval, positive findings were dominated by Rhinovirus or Enterovirus at 14.7 percent (22 detections), followed by Human Metapneumovirus at 6.7 percent (10 detections), Parainfluenza Subtype 3 at 4.7 percent (7 detections), and isolated occurrences of RSV A/B and Parainfluenza Subtype 4 at 0.7 percent each, leaving 109 samples entirely negative. Conversely, testing throughout the elevated winter illness period revealed Influenza A as the most frequent pathogen at 11.3 percent (17 detections), accompanied by Seasonal Coronavirus at 6.7 percent, Enterovirus or Rhinovirus at 4.7 percent, Human Metapneumovirus and Mycoplasma pneumoniae at 4.0 percent each, RSV A/B at 3.3 percent, Adenovirus at 2.0 percent, SARS-CoV-2 at 1.3 percent, and Parainfluenza Subtype 4 at 0.7 percent. Using empirical positivity observations, clinical actionability, and severity risk, the investigators retrospectively modeled a standardized seven-target base panel configured to maximize diagnostic yield while remaining within favorable procedural reimbursement coding thresholds. The proposed core inpatient panel encompassed SARS-CoV-2, Influenza A, Influenza B, and RSV—due to their established antiviral therapeutic indications and clinical propensity to cause life-threatening complications—supplemented by seasonal Coronavirus, Enterovirus/Rhinovirus, and Human Metapneumovirus as the most prevalent circulating pathogens. Retrospective modeling demonstrated that this baseline seven-target assembly would have successfully identified 33 of 41 positive specimens during low seasonal activity, achieving an 80.4 percent diagnostic yield, and 47 of 57 positive specimens during high seasonal activity, achieving an 82.5 percent diagnostic yield relative to standard full-panel testing. A critical advantage of the customizable molecular architecture lies in software-directed reflexive reporting, which analyzes and releases masked diagnostic targets from the initial run without requiring instrument reloading, new reagent extraction, or technologist intervention. In the inpatient study cohort, coupling the seven-target base configuration to an automatic reflex protocol for negative specimens would have resolved 80 specimens on the initial tier alone, eliminating the necessity of unmasking complete panels in those patients and driving a 27 percent overall reduction in full syndromic panel utilization across the 300 evaluated cases. The authors noted that while a baseline reflex strategy could theoretically fail to capture dual infections where one pathogen resides on the base panel and a secondary coinfection resides on the masked panel, such diagnostic risks can be managed by reserving upfront comprehensive panels for critically ill ICU patients. The study also examined the diagnostic consequences of extending the seven-target design to ambulatory settings, where clinical care currently relies on standard four-target assays. Comparing diagnostic yield against standard-of-care four-target outpatient testing demonstrated an increased chance of a diagnosis during both study periods. During the low-activity period, the seven-target configuration captured 80.5 percent of potential diagnoses compared to just 2.0 percent identified by four-target testing alone, driven largely by the high prevalence of non-targeted rhinoviruses and metapneumoviruses. During elevated winter activity, the seven-target panel yielded an 82.5 percent detection rate compared to 42.1 percent for the four-target assay. The authors emphasized that providing clinicians with a definitive viral diagnosis can actively support antibiotic stewardship initiatives by discouraging unnecessary antibacterial prescribing, provided integrated clinical decision pathways link laboratory results directly to clinical management teams. Implementing flexible testing platforms also directly addresses recurring operational inefficiencies caused by duplicative testing orders within health systems. In this study, 45 percent of patients were tested with a four-target assay within 72 hours of being tested with a BioFire RP2.1 panel. Furthermore, custom and reflexive panels would have increased the laboratory's ability to respond to two scenarios that occurred during the study period: a Bordetella pertussis outbreak and a CDC request to increase influenza subtyping. Despite these demonstrable laboratory benefits, the investigators underscored critical operational and epidemiologic considerations surrounding broad-scale adoption. Limiting diagnostic reporting to customized subsets could impair the automated transmission of pathogen surveillance data to public health registries, highlighting the need for software systems that aggregate de-identified diagnostic data in the background regardless of clinical billing tiers. Furthermore, because diagnostic performance in this investigation was derived from retrospective analysis of inpatient specimens, prospective clinical validation across diverse inpatient and outpatient cohorts remains necessary to measure direct impacts on patient outcomes, turnaround times, and total cost of care under dynamic procedural reimbursement schedules. 肺移植受者严重 RSV 结局的预测因素 - 传染病顾问

Predictors of Severe RSV Outcomes in Lung Transplant Recipients - Infectious Disease Advisor
新闻简报本报告讨论了一项回顾性 队列研究 研究,该研究分析了肺移植受者严重 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 结局的预测因素。该研究在美国 5 个移植中心以及加拿大和澳大利亚各 1 个移植中心进行,纳入了 2016 年至 2019 年间因 RSV 感染住院的 155 名成年人。主要研究结果表明,年龄较大和既往吸氧与严重后果相关,包括缺氧性呼吸衰竭和需要机械通气。研究还发现,58.7%的患者出现缺氧性呼吸衰竭,33.5%出现继发感染,17.4%需要有创机械通气。90 天全因 死亡率 率为 7.7%。该研究强调了预防策略对这一高危人群的重要性。然而,该研究存在局限性,包括其回顾性设计、潜在偏倚以及缺乏非 RSV 比较组。结果于2026年10月8日在线发表,移植与RSV感染之间的中位时间为14个月。

时间
该研究于2016年至2019年间进行,结果于2026年10月8日在线发布。移植和RSV感染之间的中位时间为14个月,90天的死亡率发生在RSV 诊断后的中位时间为21天。

该研究包括美国 5 个移植中心以及加拿大和澳大利亚各 1 个移植中心。该人群包括 155 名接受肺移植并因聚合酶链反应证实 RSV 感染而住院的成年人。
事件
进行了一项回顾性 队列研究 研究,以确定肺移植受者严重 RSV 结局的预测因子。研究发现,年龄较大和之前使用氧气与严重后果有关,例如缺氧性呼吸衰竭和需要机械通气。

Predictors of Severe RSV Outcomes in Lung Transplant Recipients - Infectious Disease Advisor

News Brief
This report discusses a retrospective cohort study analyzing predictors of severe respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) outcomes in lung transplant recipients. Conducted across 5 U.S. transplant centers and 1 each in Canada and Australia, the study included 155 adults hospitalized with RSV infection between 2016 and 2019. Key findings indicate that older age and prior oxygen use are associated with severe outcomes, including hypoxic respiratory failure and the need for mechanical ventilation. The study also found that 58.7% of patients developed hypoxic respiratory failure, 33.5% had secondary infections, and 17.4% required invasive mechanical ventilation. The 90-day all-cause mortality rate was 7.7%. The research highlights the importance of preventive strategies in this high-risk population. However, the study has limitations, including its retrospective design, potential bias, and lack of a non-RSV comparator group. The results were published online on October 8, 2026, and the median time between transplantation and RSV infection was 14 months.

Menu SECTIONS News Features CME Conferences Clinical Tools Special Collections Multimedia Fact Sheets Diagnosis & Disease Info Topics Abdominal Infections Bone and Joint Infections Cardiovascular Infections Central Nervous System Infections Covid 19 News & Insights Eye Infections Gastrointestinal Infections General Infectious Disease General Medicine Hepatitis HIV/AIDS Infection Control and Prevention Nosocomial Infections Pleuropulmonary and Bronchial Infections Practice Management Reproductive Organ Infections and Sexually Transmitted Infections Sepsis Skin and Soft Tissue Infection Upper Respiratory Tract Infections Urinary Tract Infection Vector-Borne Illnesses Haymarket Medical Network Cancer Therapy Advisor Cardiology Advisor Clinical Advisor Clinical Pain Advisor Dermatology Advisor Endocrinology Advisor Gastroenterology Advisor Hematology Advisor Infectious Disease Advisor Medical Bag MPR Neurology Advisor Obesity Advisor Oncology Nurse Advisor Ophthalmology Advisor Optometry Advisor Psychiatry Advisor Pulmonology Advisor Rare Disease Advisor Renal and Urology News Rheumatology Advisor Sleep Wake Advisor Vaccine Advisor Search for: COVID-19 News CME Drugs Calculators Charts Resources Multimedia Meetings Part of the News and Features CME Conferences Clinical Tools Special Collections Multimedia Share this article Share on Facebook Share on Twitter Share on LinkedIn Share on Bluesky Share by Email Print Pleuropulmonary and Bronchial Infections Predictors of Severe RSV Outcomes in Lung Transplant Recipients Todd Neale, MA | Publish Date November 17, 2026 Among lung transplant recipients hospitalized with respiratory syncytial virus, older age and prior oxygen use predict severe outcomes such as hypoxic respiratory failure and the need for mechanical ventilation. In lung transplant recipients who are hospitalized with respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) infection, poor outcomes are common and predicted by older age and other factors, according to study findings published in Open Forum Infectious Diseases. Recipients of solid organ transplants are vulnerable to infection in general, and those receiving new lungs are particularly susceptible to respiratory viral infections such as RSV. There is, however, limited information on risk factors for severe RSV infection outcomes in this population. To bridge this knowledge gap, researchers conducted a retrospective cohort study across 5 transplant centers in the United States and 1 each in Canada and Australia. The analysis included 155 adults (median age at RSV diagnosis, 61 [IQR, 51-67.5] years; 60% men) who underwent lung transplantation and were hospitalized with polymerase chain reaction-confirmed RSV infection between 2016 and 2019. Hospitalization for RSV occurred a median of 14 months after transplantation. More than two-thirds (68.4%) of patients received oral ribavirin, while 21.3% received inhaled ribavirin. The association between older age and severe outcomes suggests the importance of preventive strategies in this high-risk population. There was an overall decline in lung function from before to after RSV infection in a subset of patients with paired measurements — median forced expiratory volume in 1 second (FEV1) decreased from 2.01 to 1.69 L (P <.001). FEV1 declined by more than 10% in 49.2% of these patients. Regarding severe outcomes related to RSV, 58.7% of patients developed hypoxic respiratory failure, 33.5% acquired secondary fungal or bacterial respiratory infection, 17.4% required invasive mechanical ventilation during the hospital stay, and 12.3% had suspected or biopsy-proven rejection. The rate of all-cause mortality at 90 days was 7.7%, with deaths occurring a median of 21 (IQR, 16-36) days after RSV diagnosis. Logistic regression was used to explore predictors of outcomes in this population. Patients who had used oxygen prior to RSV diagnosis and those with lower respiratory tract symptoms were significantly more likely to develop hypoxic respiratory failure (odds ratios [ORs], 4.81 and 3.74, respectively). Older individuals had greater odds of requiring invasive mechanical ventilation (OR, 1.06; 95% CI, 1.02-1.12; P =.011), whereas use of oral ribavirin was associated with a lower likelihood of mechanical ventilation (OR, 0.34; 95% CI, 0.14-0.82; P =.016). In univariate analyses, use of mechanical ventilation was associated with greater odds of developing a secondary infection (OR, 3.71; 95% CI, 1.59-8.99; P =.003). Having a history of rejection in the 6 months prior to RSV infection was associated with an increased likelihood of rejection within 90 days of contracting RSV (OR, 3.23; 95% CI, 1.02-9.41; P =.035). Univariable Cox proportional hazards models revealed risk factors for 90-day mortality, including mechanical ventilation during hospitalization (hazard ratio [HR], 7.79; 95% CI, 2.47-24.60; P <.001) and time between transplantation and RSV infection (HR, 1.02; 95% CI, 1.00-1.04; P =.015). Use of oral ribavirin did not significantly influence mortality risk (HR, 5.27; 95% CI, 0.68-40.83; P =.112). Study limitations include the retrospective design, possible bias or confounding related to differences between transplant centers, the potential for residual confounding, the lack of data on RSV subtypes and ribavirin management, the absence of a non-RSV comparator group, and uncertain generalizability of the findings to nonhospitalized patients. According to the researchers, “The association between older age and severe outcomes suggests the importance of preventive strategies in this high-risk population.” References: Mendoza MA, Dumais M, Chiu CY, et al. Risk factors for hypoxia, mechanical ventilation, and mortality in lung transplant recipients hospitalized with respiratory syncytial virus: a multicenter international cohort study. Open Forum Infect Dis. Published online October 8, 2026. doi:10.1093/ofid/ofag623

Time
The study was conducted between 2016 and 2019, with results published online on October 8, 2026. The median time between transplantation and RSV infection was 14 months, and the 90-day mortality occurred a median of 21 days after RSV diagnosis.
Location and population
The study included 5 transplant centers in the United States and 1 each in Canada and Australia. The population consisted of 155 adults who underwent lung transplantation and were hospitalized with polymerase chain reaction-confirmed RSV infection.
Event
A retrospective cohort study was conducted to identify predictors of severe RSV outcomes in lung transplant recipients. The study found that older age and prior oxygen use were associated with severe outcomes such as hypoxic respiratory failure and the need for mechanical ventilation.
Scale, impact and risk
Among the 155 patients, 58.7% developed hypoxic respiratory failure, 33.5% acquired secondary fungal or bacterial respiratory infection, 17.4% required invasive mechanical ventilation, and 12.3% had suspected or biopsy-proven rejection. The 90-day all-cause mortality rate was 7.7%.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study used logistic regression and univariable Cox proportional hazards models to analyze risk factors. It acknowledged limitations such as the retrospective design, potential bias, and lack of a non-RSV comparator group.
