佐剂 RSV 疫苗 两剂量方案可增强器官移植受者的免疫力 - Bioengineer.org

Adjuvanted RSV Vaccine Two-Dose Regimen Enhances Immunity in Organ Transplant Recipients - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报一项多国 2b 期试验在成人肾和肺移植受者中评估了 AS01E 佐剂的 RSV 预融合 F 蛋白 疫苗。该研究于 2026 年 11 月 13 日发表在《自然通讯》上,发现与单剂量相比,两剂量方案显着增加了针对 RSV-A 和 RSV-B 的中和抗体滴度。该试验于 2023 年 7 月至 2025 年 5 月在澳大利亚、加拿大、德国、意大利、日本、韩国、西班牙和美国的 48 个医疗中心进行,招募了 386 名稳定的移植接受者和 125 名非免疫功能低下的成年人作为对照。由于免疫抑制治疗,移植受者出现严重 RSV 感染的风险较高,他们的初始 免疫应答 水平较低,但在第二次给药后滴度较高。该研究还指出,超过三分之一的感染 RSV 的免疫功能低下的成年人会出现肺炎和住院治疗。报告了一项严重不良事件,但申办者将其归类为无关事件。这些发现支持为实体器官移植受者开发有针对性的免疫方案。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2023 年 7 月至 2025 年 5 月期间进行,结果于 2026 年 11 月 13 日发布。主要描述性终点在第二次给药后一个月进行评估,滴度在 疫苗接种 后长达 12 个月内进行测量。
地点与对象
该试验在澳大利亚、加拿大、德国、意大利、日本、韩国、西班牙和美国的 48 个医疗中心进行。它招募了 386 名稳定的成年肾和肺移植受者,另外还有 125 名年龄在 50 岁或以上、非免疫功能低下的成年人组成的对照组。
事件
一项多国 2b 期随机试验评估了 AS01E 佐剂的 RSV 预融合 F 蛋白 疫苗 在成人肾和肺移植受者中的免疫原性、反应原性和安全性。研究发现,与单剂量相比,两剂量方案可产生更高的针对 RSV-A 和 RSV-B 的中和抗体滴度。
规模、影响与风险
该试验涉及 386 名移植接受者和 125 名非免疫功能低下的成年人。移植受者最初表现出较低的 免疫应答,但在第二次给药后获得了较高的中和抗体滴度。该研究强调了免疫功能低下个体中 RSV 感染的临床挑战,超过三分之一的感染者出现肺炎和住院治疗。
应对、状态与不确定性
该试验表明,两剂量方案改善了移植受者的 免疫应答 效果。报告了一项严重不良事件,但申办者将其归类为无关事件。该研究发表在《自然通讯》杂志上,临床试验编号为NCT05921903。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Adjuvanted RSV Vaccine Two-Dose Regimen Enhances Immunity in Organ Transplant Recipients - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A multinational phase 2b trial evaluated an AS01E-adjuvanted RSV prefusion F protein vaccine in adult kidney and lung transplant recipients. The study, published in Nature Communications on November 13, 2026, found that a two-dose regimen significantly increased neutralizing antibody titers against both RSV-A and RSV-B compared to a single dose. Conducted across 48 medical centers in Australia, Canada, Germany, Italy, Japan, the Republic of Korea, Spain, and the United States between July 2023 and May 2025, the trial enrolled 386 stable transplant recipients and 125 non-immunocompromised adults as controls. Transplant recipients, who are at higher risk for severe RSV infection due to immunosuppressive therapies, showed lower initial immune responses but achieved higher titers after the second dose. The study also noted that pneumonia and hospitalization affect over one-third of immunocompromised adults infected with RSV. One serious adverse event was reported, but it was classified as unrelated by the sponsor. The findings support the development of targeted immunization regimens for solid organ transplant recipients.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 18, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Adjuvanted RSV Vaccine Two-Dose Regimen Enhances Immunity in Organ Transplant Recipients by November 13, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Adjuvanted RSV Vaccine Two-Dose Regimen Enhances Immunity in Organ Transplant Recipients Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A multinational phase 2b randomized trial has shown that an AS01E-adjuvanted respiratory syncytial virus prefusion F protein vaccine generates substantial immune responses in adult kidney and lung transplant recipients, with a two-dose schedule providing higher neutralizing antibody titers than a single dose. Respiratory syncytial virus poses a severe threat to individuals receiving chronic maintenance immunosuppression, yet clinical trial data evaluating specialized vaccination in this vulnerable population have remained scarce. Published in Nature Communications, the investigation, cataloged as clinical trial NCT05921903, evaluated the immunogenicity, reactogenicity, and safety of the adjuvanted vaccine over twelve months. Enrolling 386 adult participants across 48 medical centers, the trial randomized immunocompromised transplant recipients to receive either one injection or two doses spaced 30 to 60 days apart, while evaluating older non-immunocompromised adults who received a single dose as controls. The trial demonstrated that transplant recipients achieved higher neutralizing antibody titers against both RSV-A and RSV-B following a second dose, supporting targeted immunization regimens in solid organ transplant medicine. Common respiratory viruses represent a formidable clinical challenge for solid organ transplant recipients, among whom pharmacological suppression of immunity amplifies the risk of progressive infection, lower respiratory tract disease, and fatal complications. Clinical observations indicate that pneumonia and inpatient hospitalization affect more than one-third of immunocompromised adults contracting the virus. Among lung transplant recipients, infection is frequently followed by debilitating sequelae; systematic reviews indicate that nearly half of infected lung recipients experience measurable declines in pulmonary function. Furthermore, these patients confront heightened vulnerabilities to allograft dysfunction, acute cellular rejection, and chronic lung allograft dysfunction. Because available therapeutic interventions for adult respiratory syncytial virus disease remain largely supportive rather than curative, preventive vaccination strategies designed to elicit protective antibodies are vital for reducing severe clinical outcomes. Developing effective immunization regimens for organ transplant recipients is complicated by the tendency of immunosuppressive therapies to blunt vaccine responsiveness, as previously observed during vaccination against influenza, pneumococcal disease, and SARS-CoV-2. The phase 2b trial addressed this obstacle by evaluating the AS01E-adjuvanted RSV prefusion F protein vaccine, which contains 120 micrograms of recombinant RSVPreF3 antigen alongside GSK’s liposome-based AS01E adjuvant system containing 25 micrograms each of 3-O-desacyl-4′-monophosphoryl lipid A and QS-21. Conducted across 48 sites in Australia, Canada, Germany, Italy, Japan, the Republic of Korea, Spain, and the United States between July 2023 and May 2025, the trial enrolled stable adult kidney and lung transplant recipients. Researchers randomized 261 transplant recipients: 131 participants were assigned to receive a single dose, whereas 130 participants were assigned to receive two doses separated by 30 to 60 days. An additional control group of 125 non-immunocompromised adults aged 50 years or older received a single vaccine dose. Demographic and clinical baseline characteristics were balanced across groups, reflecting a representative cross-section of stable transplant recipients undergoing allograft maintenance therapy. The median age of participants was 63.0 years in the single-dose transplant group, 62.0 years in the two-dose transplant group, and 64.0 years in the control group. Kidney transplant recipients represented 70.2 percent of the single-dose group and 72.3 percent of the two-dose group, with the remainder having received lung transplants. The median interval between transplantation and vaccination was 67 months in the single-dose group and 60 months in the two-dose group. Regarding baseline maintenance immunosuppressive regimens, 99.6 percent of all transplant recipients received calcineurin inhibitors or mTOR inhibitors, 79.3 percent received corticosteroids, and 76.6 percent received mycophenolate compounds. Overall, 59.4 percent of transplant recipients were maintained on triple therapy combining calcineurin inhibitors, corticosteroids, and mycophenolate compounds. Additionally, all transplant recipients had underlying medical conditions, with metabolism and nutrition disorders being the most prevalent. The primary descriptive endpoint assessed the mean geometric increase in serum neutralizing titers against RSV-A and RSV-B in the two-dose transplant group approximately one month following the second dose relative to one month following the first dose. Among the 111 participants in the per-protocol humoral immunogenicity cohort with evaluable samples at both visits, administration of the second dose consistently boosted neutralizing antibody titers. For RSV-A, the mean geometric increase post-dose two relative to post-dose one was 1.34, with a 95 percent confidence interval from 1.20 to 1.48. For RSV-B, the mean geometric increase was 1.26, with a 95 percent confidence interval extending from 1.14 to 1.40. These measurements confirmed that administering a second dose 30 to 60 days after the initial injection induced numerically higher neutralizing titers in transplant recipients than a single dose. Results across the exposed population were similar, confirming the consistency of the booster effect across participant analyses. Humoral immunogenicity kinetics revealed contrasts between organ transplant recipients and healthy control subjects following initial vaccination, alongside sustained antibody persistence throughout twelve months. Pre-vaccination neutralizing titers exceeded assay quantification thresholds in all participants, with baseline geometric mean titers ranging from 807.0 to 889.0 ED60 for RSV-A and 864.2 to 1027.3 ED60 for RSV-B. Following initial vaccination, healthy controls exhibited rapid responses, reaching titers of 6881.3 ED60 for RSV-A and 9125.2 ED60 for RSV-B at Visit 3. In contrast, transplant recipients exhibited slower initial responses, recording titers of 3990.8 ED60 for RSV-A and 5002.9 ED60 for RSV-B in the single-dose group. Group comparisons at Visit 3 showed lower initial responses in transplant recipients, with adjusted geometric mean titer ratios of controls over combined transplant groups of 1.63 for RSV-A and 1.69 for RSV-B. Following dose two, however, neutralizing titers in two-dose transplant recipients rose to 5377.9 ED60 for RSV-A and 5820.8 ED60 for RSV-B, matching control ranges. Titers waned gradually but remained above baseline at month twelve across all cohorts. Subgroup analyses provided key insights into the clinical determinants of vaccine responsiveness, highlighting the distinct effect of antimetabolite medications on humoral immunity. When stratified by transplanted organ, neutralizing antibody geometric mean titers after one and two doses were within a similar range between lung transplant recipients and kidney transplant recipients. In contrast, stratifying participants by maintenance immunosuppressive regimen revealed marked differences related to mycophenolate compound use. Transplant recipients not receiving mycophenolate compounds, comprising 23.4 percent of the transplant cohort, mounted post-dose one neutralizing titers within similar ranges to those observed in healthy controls, with a second dose conferring no additional titer increase. Conversely, transplant recipients receiving mycophenolate compounds, who represented 76.6 percent of the transplant cohort, exhibited substantially lower initial titers after a single dose. However, administering a second dose to these mycophenolate-treated participants increased neutralizing titers to ranges comparable to those observed in non-immunocompromised controls, indicating that a two-dose schedule specifically overcomes antimetabolite-associated blunting. Beyond humoral neutralizing antibodies, the trial characterized cell-mediated immune responses by evaluating antigen-specific T-cell frequencies in a pre-specified laboratory subset comprising approximately 30 percent of participants across groups. Peripheral blood mononuclear cells were stimulated with overlapping peptides spanning the RSVPreF3 sequence and evaluated using flow cytometry. Investigators quantified polypositive CD4-positive T cells expressing at least two activation markers, including CD40 ligand, 4-1BB, interleukin-2, tumor necrosis factor-alpha, interferon-gamma, interleukin-13, and interleukin-17. Baseline median frequencies of reactive CD4-positive T cells ranged between 161.0 and 239.0 cells per million CD4-positive T cells across cohorts. Following vaccination, antigen-specific CD4-positive T-cell frequencies increased above baseline in all study groups and remained elevated through twelve months, recording median frequencies between 507.0 and 1220.0 cells per million at study completion. Notably, robust CD4-positive T-cell responses appeared preserved among transplant recipients receiving mycophenolate compounds. In contrast, no antigen-specific CD8-positive T-cell response was detected. Clinical safety and reactogenicity evaluations demonstrated that both schedules exhibited an acceptable and manageable safety profile in adult organ transplant recipients. Within seven days post-dose one, solicited adverse events were reported by 80.2 percent of single-dose transplant recipients, 81.3 percent of two-dose transplant recipients, and 84.8 percent of controls, predominantly consisting of mild-to-moderate injection-site pain, fatigue, and headache lasting a median duration of three days or less. Following dose two, 77.7 percent reported solicited reactions and 2.5 percent experienced grade 3 events. Unsolicited adverse events occurred at broadly comparable frequencies across groups within 30 days of vaccination. Serious adverse events through study completion were more frequent among transplant recipients, occurring in 22.9 percent of the single-dose group and 30.0 percent of the two-dose group compared to 3.2 percent of controls, reflecting underlying medical conditions. Only one serious adverse event, gastroenteritis in a single-dose lung transplant recipient, was assessed by an investigator as vaccine-related, although the sponsor classified it as unrelated. No vaccine-related organ rejections or treatment-related deaths were observed. While these findings offer valuable clinical evidence for protecting solid organ transplant recipients, the trial investigators acknowledged several methodological limitations, including the descriptive design without hypothesis testing and the non-random selection of the cell-mediated immunogenicity subset. Furthermore, because a validated serological correlate of protection has not yet been established for respiratory syncytial virus vaccines, protective clinical efficacy must be inferred from neutralizing titers and cellular responses alongside real-world observational evidence. Nonetheless, these findings align with emerging studies, including trials evaluating mRNA-1345 in organ transplant recipients and real-world investigations like the CLEAR-VE RSV study, which demonstrated high vaccine effectiveness against hospitalization in immunocompromised populations. By demonstrating that a two-dose adjuvanted regimen overcomes antimetabolite-induced immune blunting and sustains humoral and cell-mediated immunity over twelve months without compromising allograft safety, the trial delivers compelling evidence that targeted two-dose immunization provides a favorable benefit-risk profile for kidney and lung transplant recipients facing serious seasonal respiratory virus threats. 节日期间保护婴儿免受 RSV 感染 - KSL

Protecting babies from RSV during the holiday season - KSL
新闻简报KSL 于 2026 年 11 月 13 日发表的这篇文章讨论了假日季节期间犹他州 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 病例的潜在增加。健康专家警告说,呼吸道合胞病毒是全国婴儿住院的主要原因,其在感恩节后和圣诞节前可能会增加。他们建议怀孕 32 至 36 周的准妈妈进行免疫接种,以便将保护性抗体传递给婴儿,并为符合条件的婴儿注射抗体。文章还强调了节日聚会时采取简单预防措施的重要性,例如洗手和避免与病人密切接触。在 2024-2025 年 RSV 季节期间,16,000 名犹他州婴儿得到了保护,将他们的住院风险降低了九倍。如果孩子出现呼吸困难或脱水等恶化症状,疾病预防控制中心建议寻求医疗护理。大多数保险计划免费承保 RSV 疫苗,未投保的家庭可以通过犹他州的 疫苗 儿童计划获得该疫苗。该文章作为公共卫生指导文章,强调有婴儿的家庭的预防和认识。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章于 2026 年 11 月 13 日发表,并提到感恩节后、圣诞节前(即 2026 年 11 月 24 日至 12 月 24 日左右)RSV 预计会上升。
地点与对象
本文重点关注犹他州,特别是有婴儿和准妈妈的家庭,讨论了婴儿 RSV 预防以及通过犹他州 疫苗 儿童计划获得 疫苗 的情况。
事件
犹他州的健康专家警告说,假日聚会期间呼吸道合胞病毒病例可能会增加,他们建议采取免疫和预防措施,以保护婴儿免受严重疾病的侵害。
规模、影响与风险
在 2024-2025 年 RSV 季节,犹他州有 16,000 名婴儿通过免疫接种获得了保护,使他们住院的可能性降低了九倍。文章还强调了呼吸道合胞病毒对婴儿的风险,因为它可能导致严重住院治疗。
应对、状态与不确定性
健康专家正在监测呼吸道合胞病毒病例,并建议家长咨询儿科医生的症状。建议采取免疫和预防措施,大多数保险计划免费承保 RSV 疫苗注射。

Protecting babies from RSV during the holiday season - KSL

News Brief
This article from KSL, published on November 13, 2026, discusses the potential rise in Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) cases in Utah during the holiday season. Health experts warn that RSV, a leading cause of infant hospitalization nationwide, may increase after Thanksgiving and before Christmas. They recommend immunization for expectant mothers between 32 and 36 weeks of pregnancy to pass protective antibodies to their babies, as well as antibody shots for eligible infants. The article also highlights the importance of simple precautions at holiday gatherings, such as handwashing and avoiding close contact with sick individuals. During the 2024-2025 RSV season, 16,000 Utah babies received protection, reducing their hospitalization risk by nine times. The CDC advises seeking medical care if a child shows worsening symptoms like trouble breathing or dehydration. Most insurance plans cover the RSV shot at no cost, and uninsured families can access it through Utah's Vaccines for Children program. The article serves as a public health guidance piece, emphasizing prevention and awareness for families with infants.

Entertainment Outdoors Your Life - Your Health Family Uplifting Historic Lifestyle 5B45 Kids Cost of Utah KSL Investigates Healthy Mind Matters Utah's America 250 Wednesday's Child Give-A-Thon Positively 50+ Zero Fatalities Quarters for Christmas Advertise with us Report ad Protecting babies from RSV during the holiday season Updated - Nov. 12, 2026 at 10:03 p.m. | Posted - Nov. 12, 2026 at 6:46 p.m. Emma BensonKSL 7     Save Story Leer en español Listen:2 Minutes 02:24 KEY TAKEAWAYS Health experts in Utah warn of potential RSV uptick during holiday gatherings. RSV is a leading cause of infant hospitalization; immunization offers significant protection. Parents should consult pediatricians for RSV or flu symptoms and consider preventive measures. SALT LAKE CITY — It's that time of year again. More sneezes, more coughs. Flu is already on the radar for Utah families, and with holiday gatherings coming up, health experts are also watching for RSV. For babies, even an illness that starts out looking like a cold can be harder to fight off. "Their immune system is very young. It's very immature, and they don't have the ability to fight off a lot of different viruses," said Carolyn Reynolds, executive director of the ambulatory clinical program at Intermountain Children's Health. Respiratory Syncytial Virus, or RSV, is the leading cause of hospitalization for infants nationwide. And while Reynolds said cases in Utah are low right now, she expects an uptick in the next few weeks. "I believe after Thanksgiving, before Christmas, RSV will raise its head, and we will start seeing it around," she said. RSV can look like a cold at first. Think runny nose, cough, or fever, making it hard to tell if it's RSV or the flu. Reynolds said a pediatrician can help figure out what's making your child sick. Advertise with us Report ad The CDC says to seek medical care if your child is having trouble breathing, isn't drinking enough fluids or has symptoms that are getting worse. Immunization can help protect babies from severe RSV. Expectant mothers can get the RSV vaccine between 32 and 36 weeks of pregnancy and pass protective antibodies to their babies. Eligible babies can also get an antibody shot after they're born. In the 2024-2025 RSV season, 16,000 Utah babies received protection, making them nine times less likely to end up in the hospital. And kids can get a flu shot starting at 6 months old. Families can also take simple precautions at holiday gatherings, like washing their hands often and asking guests to avoid kissing the baby's face. "Don't pass your baby around to lots of people that you don't know if they're sick or not," Reynolds said. "Take care of your kids. Do everything that you can to protect them so that you can have a really great holiday season." Parents can call their pediatrician's office or county health department to ask about a flu shot or RSV protection for their baby. Most insurance plans cover the RSV shot at no cost, and uninsured families can get it for free through Utah's Vaccines for Children program. × The Key Takeaways for this article were generated with the assistance of large language models and reviewed by our editorial team.
